The **Hollywood Walk of Fame** is a landmark with more than 2,800 five-pointed [terrazzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrazzo "Terrazzo")-and-[brass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brass "Brass") stars embedded in the sidewalks along fifteen blocks of [Hollywood Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Boulevard "Hollywood Boulevard") and three blocks of [Vine Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vine_Street "Vine Street") in the [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_Los_Angeles "Hollywood, Los Angeles") district of [Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles "Los Angeles"), California, United States. The stars are monuments to achievement in the entertainment industry and bear the names of a mix of actors, filmmakers, musicians, inventors, businessmen, fictional characters, and more. Many individuals have been honored more than once.
The Walk of Fame was originally conceived in 1953. The first stars were unveiled in 1958, and the first permanent stars were placed in 1960. The Walk has been expanded on multiple fronts since then, including in the number of stars displayed; [categories, entities, and organizations honored](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_stars_on_the_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame "List of stars on the Hollywood Walk of Fame"); and blocks covered. The Walk also deteriorated while it expanded, and a long-term restoration plan began in 2007.
The Walk of Fame is administered by the [Hollywood Chamber of Commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Chamber_of_Commerce "Hollywood Chamber of Commerce") and maintained by the self-financing Hollywood Historic Trust. The Chamber evaluates nominations through a selection committee and upon acceptance, it collects fees from the new honoree's sponsor to fund the creation and installation of their star as well as maintenance for the entire Walk. The Chamber also owns the Walk's licensing rights.
The Walk of Fame is one of Los Angeles's most popular tourist attractions, receiving an estimated ten million annual visitors in 2010. Attendance at new star unveilings can reach into the thousands, and the Walk is also a popular site for street performers, tributes, and protests. Some of the Walk's entries are controversial, as is its overall state, which leaves many disappointed and has earned it several pejoratives. Despite this, the Walk has inspired [other walks around the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_halls_and_walks_of_fame#Walks_of_fame "List of halls and walks of fame").
## Description
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=1 "Edit section: Description")]
The Walk of Fame spans 1.3 miles (2.1km) on [Hollywood Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Boulevard "Hollywood Boulevard") between [Gower Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gower_Street_\(Los_Angeles\) "Gower Street \(Los Angeles\)") and [La Brea Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Brea_Avenue "La Brea Avenue"), and 0.4 miles (0.64km) on [Vine Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vine_Street "Vine Street") between [Yucca Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yucca_Corridor,_Los_Angeles "Yucca Corridor, Los Angeles") and [Sunset Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunset_Boulevard "Sunset Boulevard").[[1]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-1)
[![Mary Pickford's Walk of Fame star](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/77/Mary_Pickford_star_on_the_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame%2C_Hollywood%2C_California_LCCN2013632765.tif/lossy-page1-250px-Mary_Pickford_star_on_the_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame%2C_Hollywood%2C_California_LCCN2013632765.tif.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Mary_Pickford_star_on_the_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame,_Hollywood,_California_LCCN2013632765.tif)A star in the motion pictures category
As of September2026[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit)[[2]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-wof_pr-2) all but one of which are placed in the ground,[[3]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ali-3) and all but the Awards of Excellence are set into the sidewalk at 6-foot (1.8m) intervals.[[4]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ign-4)[[5]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-disneylandorg-5) The stars are five-point, coral-pink [terrazzo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrazzo "Terrazzo") rimmed with [brass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brass "Brass")[[4]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ign-4)[[6]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-discover-6) and inlaid into a 3-by-3-foot (0.91 by 0.91m)[[7]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-specs-7)[[8]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-mta-8) charcoal-colored terrazzo background.[[9]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-stardesc-9) Inside the stars, honorees' names are embedded with center-arranged 2-inch (5.1cm) brass letters in one or two rows, and below each name is a circular 6-inch (15cm) brass emblem that corresponds with the category the honoree is honored in.[[7]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-specs-7)[[10]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-howmade-10) Stars face differing directions so that pedestrians can see them easily from whichever direction they are walking.[[11]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-WOFFAQ-11) The Walk also contains hundreds of blank stars, each serving as a placeholder for a future honoree.[[12]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LATabout-12)
### Categorization
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=2 "Edit section: Categorization")]
Stars on the Walk of Fame are awarded in six categories: [motion pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film "Film"), [television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrestrial_television "Terrestrial television"), [recording](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sound_recording_and_reproduction "Sound recording and reproduction"), [radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radio_broadcasting "Radio broadcasting"), [live theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theater "Theater")/[performance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Performance "Performance"), and [sports entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport "Sport").[[13]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-BB-13)[[14]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-14)[[Note 1]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-16) Their emblems are:
>   * [![Circular 4-inch brass plaque showing a side view of a classic movie camera.](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Walk_of_Fame_Category_Motion_Pictures_2.png/60px-Walk_of_Fame_Category_Motion_Pictures_2.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Walk_of_Fame_Category_Motion_Pictures_2.png) **Classic film camera** , representing motion pictures[[15]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-michigan-15)
>   * [![Circular 4-inch brass plaque with a tube-type television with twin aerials.](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Walk_of_Fame_Category_Television.png/60px-Walk_of_Fame_Category_Television.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Walk_of_Fame_Category_Television.png) **Television** , representing television[[15]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-michigan-15)
>   * [![Circular 4-inch brass plaque with a top view of phonograph disc and pickup arm.](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Walk_of_Fame_Category_Recording.png/60px-Walk_of_Fame_Category_Recording.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Walk_of_Fame_Category_Recording.png) **Phonograph record** , representing recording[[15]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-michigan-15)
>   * [![Circular 4-inch brass plaque with an antique studio-style microphone.](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Walk_of_Fame_Category_Radio.png/60px-Walk_of_Fame_Category_Radio.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Walk_of_Fame_Category_Radio.png) **Radio microphone** , representing radio[[15]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-michigan-15)
>   * [![Circular 4-inch brass plaque with the classic theatrical comedy/tragedy masks.](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Walk_of_Fame_Category_Theater.png/60px-Walk_of_Fame_Category_Theater.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Walk_of_Fame_Category_Theater.png) **Comedy/tragedy masks** , representing live theater/performance[[15]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-michigan-15)
>   * [![Circular 4-inch brass plaque with athletic trophy](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/Walk_of_Fame_Category_Athletic_trophy.png/60px-Walk_of_Fame_Category_Athletic_trophy.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Walk_of_Fame_Category_Athletic_trophy.png) **Athletic trophy** , representing sports entertainment[[16]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-17)
> 

A seventh special category exists for the [Hollywood Chamber of Commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Chamber_of_Commerce "Hollywood Chamber of Commerce") to directly honor organizations, corporations, and more.[[15]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-michigan-15) These entries, known as Awards of Excellence,[[13]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-BB-13)[[17]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-18) do not feature the category emblems and their text is in a different font.[[13]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-BB-13)
The categories are not represented equally. As of October2025[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit)[all stars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_stars_on_the_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame "List of stars on the Hollywood Walk of Fame") were in the motion pictures category, 25% in television, 19% in recording, 9% in radio, fewer than 3% in live theater/performance, and fewer than 1% in sports entertainment and Awards of Excellence combined.[[Note 2]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-20)
### Alternative designs
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=3 "Edit section: Alternative designs")]
[![Moon Landing monument, with square pink terrazzo pad, with half light, half medium-dark gray terrazzo Moon disk showing TV emblem at top and brass lettering inlaid that reads: Neil A Armstrong / Edwin E Aldrin Jr / Michael Collins / 7/20/69 / Apollo / XI](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/HollywoodWalkOfFameMoonAtHollywoodAndVine.jpg/250px-HollywoodWalkOfFameMoonAtHollywoodAndVine.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:HollywoodWalkOfFameMoonAtHollywoodAndVine.jpg)One of four monuments recognizing the [Apollo 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_11 "Apollo 11") astronauts
Awards of Excellence do not use the same font or display the same emblems as the rest of the Walk.[[13]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-BB-13) [Johnny Grant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnny_Grant_\(radio_personality\) "Johnny Grant \(radio personality\)")'s Award of Excellence emblem, for example, displays the [seal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seal_\(emblem\) "Seal \(emblem\)") of Hollywood,[[19]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-21)[[20]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-JGrant-22) and the emblem on the [L.A.P.D. Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Division "Hollywood Division") star displays a Hollywood Division [badge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badge "Badge").[[21]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-23)[[22]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-24) Furthermore, Awards of Excellence for Walk of Fame sponsors [Absolut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absolut "Absolut"),[[23]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LATAbs-25) [L'Oréal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'Or%C3%A9al "L'Oréal"),[[24]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-susko-26) and [Princess Cruises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Princess_Cruises "Princess Cruises") and _[The Love Boat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Love_Boat "The Love Boat")_[[25]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-27) contain "mini stars".[[24]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-susko-26)
The Walk's [Apollo 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_11 "Apollo 11") entries are circular and meant to resemble the [Moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon "Moon"). They also display the date of the [Moon landing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moon_landing#Human_Moon_landings "Moon landing"), "Apollo XI", and their color scheme is silver and grey on a pink background. The Walk contains four of these entries, one on each corner of [Hollywood and Vine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_and_Vine "Hollywood and Vine").[[26]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-military-28)[[27]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-specialapollo-29)[[28]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LATApollo-30)
### Star locations
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=4 "Edit section: Star locations")]
[![Pedestrians looking at two rows of alternating stars in the charcoal sidewalk on the north side of Hollywood Boulevard](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7a/Hollywood%2C_Los_Angeles%2C_CA%2C_USA_-_panoramio_%2831%29.jpg/250px-Hollywood%2C_Los_Angeles%2C_CA%2C_USA_-_panoramio_%2831%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Hollywood,_Los_Angeles,_CA,_USA_-_panoramio_\(31\).jpg)Stars outside [Grauman's Chinese Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grauman's_Chinese_Theatre "Grauman's Chinese Theatre")
The blocks outside [Grauman's Chinese Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grauman's_Chinese_Theatre "Grauman's Chinese Theatre") and [Hollywood Roosevelt Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Roosevelt_Hotel "Hollywood Roosevelt Hotel") are considered the prime locations for stars.[[29]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-starplaces-31) Stars of particularly well-known celebrities are often placed in front of the Chinese,[[30]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-wandp-32) Oscar winners' stars near [Dolby Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolby_Theatre "Dolby Theatre"),[[30]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-wandp-32)[[31]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Q&A-33) [Disney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney "Disney") characters outside [El Capitan Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Capitan_Theatre "El Capitan Theatre"),[[32]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ciclavia-34) [rock 'n' rollers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_'n'_roll "Rock 'n' roll") outside the [Musicians Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Musicians_Institute "Musicians Institute"),[[33]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-35) and other musicians outside the [Capitol Records Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_Records_Building "Capitol Records Building").[[32]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ciclavia-34) Family members and co-stars are often placed together.[[34]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-people-36)
Hollywood Chamber of Commerce producer Ana Martinez has decided star locations since c.1987[[Note 3]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-38) and tries to choose somewhere connected to the honoree.[[34]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-people-36) For example, [John Waters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Waters "John Waters") and [Ray Bradbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ray_Bradbury "Ray Bradbury")'s stars were placed outside [Larry Edmunds Bookshop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_Edmunds_Bookshop "Larry Edmunds Bookshop"), a business the former has said he loved and the latter had patronized for fifty years;[[36]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-39)[[37]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-40) [Tim Burton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Burton "Tim Burton")'s star was placed outside [Hollywood Toys & Costumes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Toys_&_Costumes "Hollywood Toys & Costumes"), a business he credits as one of his childhood inspirations;[[38]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-41) and [Farrah Fawcett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farrah_Fawcett "Farrah Fawcett")'s star was placed outside a [hair salon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hair_salon "Hair salon"), as she was known for her hair.[[10]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-howmade-10) Some stars were placed in locations connected to the honoree before Martinez was in charge as well, for example [Alfred Hitchcock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Hitchcock "Alfred Hitchcock")'s star was placed outside [Fox Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Hollywood "Fox Hollywood"), a theater that was showing his film _[Psycho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psycho_\(1960_film\) "Psycho \(1960 film\)")_ at the time of the unveiling.[[39]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-hwoodbook-42)
For actors, stars are sometimes placed in locations connected to a role they are known for. Examples include [Austin Powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austin_Powers_\(film_series\) "Austin Powers \(film series\)") actor [Mike Myers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Myers "Mike Myers"), whose star was placed outside an adult store called the International Love Boutique;[[4]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ign-4)[[40]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-43) [James Bond 007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Bond_007 "James Bond 007") actors [Roger Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Moore "Roger Moore") and [Daniel Craig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Craig "Daniel Craig"), whose stars are located at 7007 Hollywood Boulevard;[[41]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-44) _[24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/24_\(TV_series\) "24 \(TV series\)")_ star [Kiefer Sutherland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiefer_Sutherland "Kiefer Sutherland"), whose star is located at 7024 Hollywood Boulevard;[[42]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-45) [Ed O'Neill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_O'Neill "Ed O'Neill"), whose star was placed outside a shoe store in reference to his character's occupation on _[Married... with Children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Married..._with_Children "Married... with Children")_ ;[[43]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-46) and [Steve Guttenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Guttenberg "Steve Guttenberg"), whose star was placed outside the Police Activities League in reference to his role in the [Police Academy franchise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_Academy_\(franchise\) "Police Academy \(franchise\)").[[34]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-people-36)
Honorees may work with the Chamber to find a location for their star.[[31]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Q&A-33) Those whose stars were placed at a requested location include [Jay Leno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jay_Leno "Jay Leno"), who requested a spot near Hollywood and Highland because twenty years prior he had been arrested for [vagrancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vagrancy_\(people\) "Vagrancy \(people\)") there twice;[[44]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-47) [George Carlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Carlin "George Carlin"), who requested his star be placed in front of [KDAY](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KDAY "KDAY") radio station, where he was previously employed;[[45]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-48) [Ed McMahon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_McMahon "Ed McMahon"), who requested his star be next to [W. C. Fields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W._C._Fields "W. C. Fields")'s;[[31]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Q&A-33) and [Carol Burnett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carol_Burnett "Carol Burnett"), who requested her star be placed outside [Warner Brothers Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Pacific_Theatre "Hollywood Pacific Theatre"), where she had worked as an usherette and was fired for insisting a couple arriving during the final few minutes of a screening wait for the next screening.[[46]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Burnett-49) The Chamber typically does not accommodate requests for spots outside the Chinese Theatre, as that area is reserved for "show-biz royalty",[[31]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Q&A-33) however, when [Michael Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jackson "Michael Jackson") refused to attend his unveiling unless his star was located in front of the Chinese, the Chamber acquiesced to his demand.[[47]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-itisnteasy-50)
Awards of Excellence are not located on the Walk, as many feature commercial names, which are banned on sidewalks in Los Angeles. Instead, these entries are located on private property next to the Walk, sometimes just inches away.[[5]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-disneylandorg-5)[[13]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-BB-13)[[23]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LATAbs-25)
### Other elements
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=5 "Edit section: Other elements")]
[![side view of the gazebo showing the ground, the structure, the caryatids, the spire, and the letters "Hollywood". The top of the spire extends beyond the image](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/5/52/Four_Ladies_of_Hollywood_2008.jpg/250px-Four_Ladies_of_Hollywood_2008.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Four_Ladies_of_Hollywood_2008.jpg)_Hollywood and La Brea Gateway_ , also known as the _Four Ladies of Hollywood_
In addition to stars, the Walk consists of several other elements. _Hollywood and La Brea Gateway_ , also known as _Four Ladies of Hollywood_ ,[[48]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Fry-51) is a [gazebo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gazebo "Gazebo") located on a traffic island at Hollywood Boulevard and La Brea Avenue[[49]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Art-52) that serves as a gateway to the Walk.[[48]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Fry-51) Created in 1993 and dedicated on February 1, 1994,[[49]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Art-52) the structure was designed by [Catherine Hardwicke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_Hardwicke "Catherine Hardwicke") and consists of a square stainless steel [Art Deco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Deco "Art Deco")-style open roof held up by life-size [caryatids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caryatid "Caryatid") of [Dorothy Dandridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dorothy_Dandridge "Dorothy Dandridge"), [Anna May Wong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_May_Wong "Anna May Wong"), [Dolores del Río](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolores_del_R%C3%ADo "Dolores del Río"), and [Mae West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mae_West "Mae West"), each standing on a square base and wearing a glamorous gown. The gazebo's roof is rounded and supports a circular dome, the dome topped by a central [spire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spire "Spire") with descending neon block letters spelling "Hollywood" on each side. Atop the spire is a small [gilded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilding "Gilding") [weather vane-style sculpture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weather_vane "Weather vane") of [Marilyn Monroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marilyn_Monroe "Marilyn Monroe") in her billowing skirt pose from _[The Seven Year Itch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Seven_Year_Itch "The Seven Year Itch")_.[[48]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Fry-51)[[49]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Art-52)[[50]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-53) Critics have described the gazebo as "depressingly awful"[[51]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-54) and "creative and wonderful."[[52]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-55)
The _Hollywood Walk of Fame Mural_ is a 100-foot (30m) [mixed-media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mixed-media "Mixed-media") [mural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mural "Mural") made to celebrate the Walk's 60th anniversary and the [Chamber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Chamber_of_Commerce "Hollywood Chamber of Commerce")'s 100th. The mural is meant to tell "a comprehensive story of the history of Walk of Fame from its creation to its persistence..." and also "captur[e] the ages, the diversity and the beauty of the Walk of Fame and its recipients..." It was created by Ian Robertson-Salt, curated by Art Share LA, and unveiled on June 11, 2021.[[53]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-56)[[54]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-57)
A Walk of Fame [time capsule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_capsule "Time capsule") commemorating the Walk's 50th anniversary was buried below a specially-designed star near Hollywood and Highland in 2010. The capsule contains more than fifty items, including a signed picture of [Joanne Woodward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joanne_Woodward "Joanne Woodward"), a piece of [Elvis Presley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elvis_Presley "Elvis Presley")'s star, a replica of the [Capitol Records building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_Records_building "Capitol Records building"), [Emmy award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmy_award "Emmy award") memorabilia, an assortment of [DVDs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DVDs "DVDs"), the script for _[Casablanca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casablanca_\(film\) "Casablanca \(film\)")_ , playbills from [The Pantages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Pantages "Hollywood Pantages"), news articles about the Walk of Fame, and a note from [Bob Barker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Barker "Bob Barker"). It is scheduled to be opened in 2060.[[55]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-time_capsule-58)
[![Bob Hope's live performance star with a rectangular plaque beside it that reads "The Hollywood Chamber of Commerce / honors / BOB HOPE / Hollywood's Citizen of the Century](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/Bob_Hope_Star_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_%2851182074649%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Bob_Hope_Star_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_%2851182074649%29_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bob_Hope_Star_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_\(51182074649\)_\(cropped\).jpg)[Bob Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Hope "Bob Hope")'s live performance star with a plaque honoring him as Hollywood's Citizen of the Century
Some stars are accompanied by plaques about the star or its recipient. For example, a plaque beside [Bob Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Hope "Bob Hope")'s live performance star honors him as Hollywood's Citizen of the Century, a designation he was given when the star was re-dedicated for his 100th birthday;[[56]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-59) a plaque beside [Gene Autry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene_Autry "Gene Autry")'s live performance star, his fifth star awarded,[[57]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-60) notes that he is the only individual honored in all (at the time) five categories;[[58]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-61) and a plaque beside [Jennifer Lopez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Lopez "Jennifer Lopez")'s star recognizes it as the 2,500th star unveiled.[[59]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-62) Additionally, stars unveiled in 2010 are accompanied by a 50th anniversary plaque.[[60]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-twincities-63)
Walk of Fame honorees also receive a replica plaque for their personal ownership.[[61]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-janetlee-64)[[62]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-diversity-65)
## Administration
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=6 "Edit section: Administration")]
The [Hollywood Chamber of Commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Chamber_of_Commerce "Hollywood Chamber of Commerce") administers the Walk of Fame, which is maintained by the self-financing Hollywood Historic Trust. The Chamber collects fees ($85,000 as of 2026[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit)[[11]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-WOFFAQ-11)[[24]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-susko-26)[[62]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-diversity-65) The Chamber also owns the Walk's licensing rights,[[63]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-66) which they have upheld through [cease and desist orders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cease_and_desist_order "Cease and desist order") and lawsuits.[[64]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-67)[[65]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-68)[[66]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-trademarklawsuit-69) These licensing rights earned the Chamber an estimated $500,000 from souvenirs in 2012 ($701,189in 2025).[[29]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-starplaces-31)
### Nominations
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=7 "Edit section: Nominations")]
In 1962, the City of Los Angeles specified the Hollywood Chamber of Commerce as the organization to advise them about new entries to the Walk.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70) The Chamber does so through a selection committee that receives between 200 and 300 nominations per year.[[11]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-WOFFAQ-11)[[61]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-janetlee-64) Anyone, including fans, can nominate anyone other than themselves as long as the nominee approves.[[11]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-WOFFAQ-11)[[31]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Q&A-33)[[68]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Jet111-71) A minimum of five years' experience in the nominated category is required and posthumous nominees must have been deceased for at least two years;[[13]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-BB-13) posthumous nominees also require the approval of the nominee's family or [estate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estate_\(law\) "Estate \(law\)").[[69]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-prince-72) The nomination fee is $275 as of 2026[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit)[[70]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-WOFFAQforNoms-73)
[![James Cameron squats by his new star, left hand propping up his plaque](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/JamesCameronStarDec09.jpg/250px-JamesCameronStarDec09.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:JamesCameronStarDec09.jpg)[James Cameron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Cameron "James Cameron") unveiling his star in 2009
Nominees are evaluated based on professional achievement (including awards and other honors), charitable contributions, and longevity.[[68]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Jet111-71) The selection process begins in April,[[61]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-janetlee-64) and by June, approximately thirty nominations are selected.[[11]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-WOFFAQ-11) All selections must be signed off by [Los Angeles City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_City_Council "Los Angeles City Council") and the [Board of Public Works](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Los_Angeles "Government of Los Angeles"),[[71]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-garrison-74) and once selected, honorees must schedule a ceremony within two years or their nomination is rejected.[[11]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-WOFFAQ-11)
Traditionally, selection committee members other than chairman are not made public. However, in 1999, in response to intensifying charges of secrecy in the selection process, the Chamber disclosed its committee members; they were: [Johnny Grant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnny_Grant_\(radio_personality\) "Johnny Grant \(radio personality\)"), committee chair; [Earl Lestz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earl_Lestz "Earl Lestz"), president of [Paramount Studio Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Pictures "Paramount Pictures"); Stan Spero, retired manager at [KMPC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KMPC "KMPC") and [KABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KABC_\(AM\) "KABC \(AM\)"); Kate Nelson, owner of [Palace Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avalon_Hollywood "Avalon Hollywood"); and Mary Lou Dudas, vice president of [A&M Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A&M_Records "A&M Records").[[72]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-75) As of 2018[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit)[[73]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-reilly-76)
Johnny Grant remained committee chair until his death in January 2008[[74]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-houseofreps-77) and by September, Lestz was chair.[[75]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-78) By 2010, former Director of Communications for the [Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Motion_Picture_Arts_and_Sciences "Academy of Motion Picture Arts and Sciences") John Pavlik was chairman,[[76]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-79)[[77]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-80) while David Green was chair in 2012 and 2013,[[78]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-81) Maureen Schultz in 2014,[[79]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-82) [Vin Di Bona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vin_Di_Bona "Vin Di Bona") in 2016 through 2019,[[80]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-83) [Ellen K](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellen_K "Ellen K") in 2020 through 2023,[[81]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-84) and [Peter Roth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Roth_\(executive\) "Peter Roth \(executive\)") in 2024 and 2025.[[82]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-85) [Kristen Chenoweth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kristen_Chenoweth "Kristen Chenoweth") and [LL Cool J](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LL_Cool_J "LL Cool J") were reported to be committee members in 2018,[[73]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-reilly-76) and the Chamber's current position is that "each of the six categories is represented by Walk of Famers with expertise in that field."[[11]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-WOFFAQ-11)
### Rule adjustments
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=8 "Edit section: Rule adjustments")]
[![Muhammad Ali's star, a live performance emblem below his name, on a single black terrazzo slab with a metal border, the border surrounded by tan wall slabs, with a row of tile fillet where the wall meets the black terrazzo slab-covered floor](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Muhammad_Ali%27s_star_on_the_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame.jpg/250px-Muhammad_Ali%27s_star_on_the_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Muhammad_Ali's_star_on_the_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame.jpg)[Muhammad Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ali "Muhammad Ali")'s star in the live performance category, placed vertically in a wall so it cannot be walked upon
Rules prohibit nominees whose contributions lie outside the categories the Walk of Fame honors, but the Selection Committee has adjusted interpretations of its categories to justify selections. For example, the [Apollo 11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollo_11 "Apollo 11") monuments officially recognize the astronauts for "contributions to the television industry", something that committee chairman Johnny Grant acknowledged was "a bit of a stretch."[[31]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Q&A-33) Another example is [Muhammad Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Ali "Muhammad Ali"), whose star was granted after the committee decided [boxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boxing "Boxing") was a form of live performance; Ali's star was also placed vertically in a wall instead of horizontally in the sidewalk because Ali shares his name with the Islamic prophet [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad "Muhammad") and therefore requested it not be walked upon.[[3]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ali-3)[[83]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-86)
Only one honoree has had their star unveiled after failing to attend the required unveiling: [Barbra Streisand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbra_Streisand "Barbra Streisand"). Streisand did attend when her husband [James Brolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Brolin "James Brolin") was honored two decades later.[[4]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ign-4)
### Upkeep
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=9 "Edit section: Upkeep")]
According to local law, the city of Los Angeles bears no responsibility for the maintenance of sidewalks, including those of the Hollywood Walk of Fame. Instead, the responsibility lies with property owners, or in the case of the Walk of Fame, a property owners' association. As such, maintenance for the Walk of Fame is paid for by the [Hollywood Chamber of Commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Chamber_of_Commerce "Hollywood Chamber of Commerce"), funded by half of the fee for each new star. However, these funds are not used exclusively for cleaning and repairs; for example, they also pay for streetscape holiday lighting.[[84]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-baum-87)
As of 2016[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit)6,700 and $13,400 in 2025). The Chamber often waits for private developers to pay for this, as it is usually included in neighboring construction projects. The Chamber also prioritizes repairing serious damage, such as trip-and-fall hazards, over cosmetic repairs.[[84]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-baum-87)
The Star Polishers, a group of volunteers who shined the Walk's stars, was formed c.1980[[Note 4]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-89) and the group had reached 140 members by 1995. Also in 1995, the City of Los Angeles cleaned the Walk nightly via high-powered hot water washing; this cleaning, however, did not polish or shine the stars.[[85]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-starpolishers-88) In 1999, the [Hollywood Business Improvement District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Business_improvement_districts_in_the_United_States "Business improvement districts in the United States") hired homeless, one-legged, former television repairman John Peterson to polish every star at least once a month; previously, he had been doing so of his own initiative.[[86]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-jpeterson-90)[[87]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-91) Peterson retired in 2014,[[86]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-jpeterson-90) after which his position was filled by a rotating crew of contractors, and as of 2019[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit)[[88]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-92)
### Star removal
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=10 "Edit section: Star removal")]
The idea of removing stars was broached as early as 1958; however, no star has ever been removed from the Walk.[[89]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-walkofinfamy-93) In answer to various requests, the Chamber announced in 2017 that "the Hollywood Walk of Fame is a registered historic landmark. Once a star has been added ... it is considered a part of the historic fabric"[[Note 5]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-96) and will not be removed.[[92]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-star.removal-97) The following year Chamber president [Leron Gubler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leron_Gubler?action=edit&redlink=1 "Leron Gubler \(page does not exist\)") stated “we view [the Walk of Fame] as part of history, and we don’t erase history.”[[73]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-reilly-76) The Chamber further clarified its position in 2024, stating that it does not have the authority to remove stars.[[93]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-cananyone-98)
## History
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=11 "Edit section: History")]
### Origin
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=12 "Edit section: Origin")]
The Walk of Fame was conceived in response to the significant revenue drops [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_California "Hollywood, California") was experiencing in the late 1940s and early 1950s, caused by [suburbanization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suburbanization "Suburbanization") and the [Paramount Decree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Decree "Paramount Decree").[[94]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-99percent-99) It was created to honor figures in the entertainment industry, engage tourists, and improve Hollywood's streets.[[95]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-100)
Some sources give [Hollywood Chamber of Commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Chamber_of_Commerce "Hollywood Chamber of Commerce") member and Hollywood Improvement Association president Harry Sugarman credit for coming up with the idea of the Walk,[[66]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-trademarklawsuit-69)[[96]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LA_Magazine-101) while the Chamber itself credits 1953 volunteer president E. M. Stuart and also states that the Hollywood Improvement Association was established with Sugarman as president to see the project through to completion.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70) The origin of the star concept is also uncertain, although it may have come from stars that displayed celebrity names on [Hollywood Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Hotel "Hollywood Hotel")'s dining room ceiling;[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70)[[97]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-102) another theory has it coming from Sugarman's Tropics, a restaurant whose drink menu featured celebrity photos framed in gold stars.[[96]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LA_Magazine-101) Regardless, by 1955, the basic idea had been agreed upon; it was submitted to [City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_City_Council "Los Angeles City Council") in January 1956.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70)
Between May 1956 and fall 1957, 1,558 honorees were selected by committees representing four branches of the entertainment industry: motion pictures, television, recording, and radio. Committee members included [Cecil B. DeMille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cecil_B._DeMille "Cecil B. DeMille"), [Samuel Goldwyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Goldwyn "Samuel Goldwyn"), [Jesse L. Lasky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesse_L._Lasky "Jesse L. Lasky"), [Walt Disney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Disney "Walt Disney"), [Hal Roach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hal_Roach "Hal Roach"), [Mack Sennett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mack_Sennett "Mack Sennett"), and [Walter Lantz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Lantz "Walter Lantz").[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70) The recording committee originally required minimum sales of one million [records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_record "Music record") or 250,000 [albums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_album "Music album"), but they soon realized many important artists would be excluded by this requirement. As a result, the [National Academy of Recording Arts and Sciences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Academy_of_Recording_Arts_and_Sciences "National Academy of Recording Arts and Sciences") was formed to create a separate award for the music industry, leading to the first [Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grammy_Award "Grammy Award") in 1959.[[98]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-103)
In February 1956, a prototype was unveiled featuring [John Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Wayne "John Wayne")'s name and [caricature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caricature "Caricature") inside a star; caricatures, however, proved too difficult to execute and were replaced by brass emblems.[[4]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ign-4)[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70) The Walk's proposal also originally called for a blue and brown sidewalk, but this was vetoed by [Charles E. Toberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_E._Toberman "Charles E. Toberman"), reportedly because it clashed with a building he had under construction.[[4]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ign-4)[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70) The final design, including a coral-and-charcoal color scheme,[[99]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-fromthearchives-104) was then approved, and in September 1958, eight prototype stars were installed on the northwest corner of Hollywood and Highland,[[100]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Wana-105)[[101]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-storyof-106) their purpose to generate publicity, gauge public interest, and demonstrate how the Walk would look.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70)[[102]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-mildside-107) The eight honored, chosen at random, were [Olive Borden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olive_Borden "Olive Borden"), [Ronald Colman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Colman "Ronald Colman"), [Louise Fazenda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louise_Fazenda "Louise Fazenda"), [Preston Foster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preston_Foster "Preston Foster"), [Burt Lancaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burt_Lancaster "Burt Lancaster"), [Edward Sedgwick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Sedgwick "Edward Sedgwick"), [Ernest Torrence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernest_Torrence "Ernest Torrence"), and [Joanne Woodward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joanne_Woodward "Joanne Woodward").[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70)[[100]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Wana-105)[[102]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-mildside-107) Of these eight, Joanne Woodward is often singled out as the first honoree because she was the first to pose with her star for photographs.[[31]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Q&A-33)
[![A woman in a red dress points at Charlton Heston's star with other stars, Hollywood Brown Derby, Vine Street, and the Dyas Building viewable behind it. The text "Star-Sidewalks / Hollywood, Calif." printed in a red cursive font at the top of the image](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Hollywood_and_Vine_star_studded_sidewalks_1960s_%288349105259%29.png/250px-Hollywood_and_Vine_star_studded_sidewalks_1960s_%288349105259%29.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Hollywood_and_Vine_star_studded_sidewalks_1960s_\(8349105259\).png)[Vine Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vine_Street "Vine Street") near [Hollywood and Vine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_and_Vine "Hollywood and Vine") in the 1960s
Construction of the Walk was scheduled to begin in 1958, but two lawsuits delayed it. The first was filed by local property owners challenging the $85 per foot of storefront, $1.25 million ($13.9millionin 2025) in total,[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70) tax assessment levied upon them.[[103]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-lawsuits-108) In 1959, the assessment was ruled legal.[[104]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-109) The second was filed by [Charles Chaplin Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Chaplin_Jr. "Charles Chaplin Jr."), who sought $400,000 ($4.46millionin 2025) in damages for the exclusion of [his father](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Chaplin "Charlie Chaplin"),[[103]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-lawsuits-108) whose inclusion had been withdrawn due to pressure from local business owners.[[12]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LATabout-12) Chaplin Jr.'s suit was dismissed in 1960, allowing for construction to begin.[[105]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-110)
The Walk's official groundbreaking took place on February 8, 1960, and was attended by Chamber members Stuart and Sugarman, [Los Angeles County Board of Supervisors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_Board_of_Supervisors "Los Angeles County Board of Supervisors") member [Ernest E. Debs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernest_E._Debs "Ernest E. Debs"), and future honorees [Linda Darnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linda_Darnell "Linda Darnell"), [Gigi Perreau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigi_Perreau "Gigi Perreau"), [Francis X. Bushman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_X._Bushman "Francis X. Bushman"), and [Charles Coburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Coburn "Charles Coburn").[[12]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LATabout-12) On March 28, 1960, the first permanent star, [Stanley Kramer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Kramer "Stanley Kramer")'s, was set near Hollywood and Gower.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70)[[106]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-111) In total, 1,558 names were set for honorees,[[71]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-garrison-74) as were about 500 blank stars meant to acknowledge the Walk as a living monument.[[94]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-99percent-99) The Walk was dedicated on November 23, 1960, and completed the following spring.[[74]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-houseofreps-77)
### Stagnation and revitalization
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=13 "Edit section: Stagnation and revitalization")]
The 1960s and 1970s saw protracted [urban decay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_decay "Urban decay") in Hollywood.[[107]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-112)[[108]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Vincent1-113) After the Walk's initial installation, eight years passed without the addition of a new star. In 1962, the [Los Angeles City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_City_Council "Los Angeles City Council") passed an ordinance naming the Hollywood Chamber of Commerce the agent to advise the City regarding new honorees. The Chamber then devised rules, procedures, and financing methods to do so. In December 1968, [Richard D. Zanuck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_D._Zanuck "Richard D. Zanuck") was awarded the Walk's first new star[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70)[[109]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-114) and by May 1975, 99 stars had been added.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70)[[74]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-houseofreps-77) The 1970s also saw the Chamber switch from anointing honorees to requiring nominations.[[71]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-garrison-74)
[![Johnny Grant, wearing glasses and a dark blue suit, and Joe Pasternak, also wearing a dark blue suit, hold a Walk of Fame award that features a replica star as a crowd around them clap and look on](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Joe_Pasternak_Allan_Warren.jpg/250px-Joe_Pasternak_Allan_Warren.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Joe_Pasternak_Allan_Warren.jpg)[Johnny Grant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnny_Grant_\(radio_personality\) "Johnny Grant \(radio personality\)"), center, at [Joe Pasternak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Pasternak "Joe Pasternak")'s star unveiling, 1991
In 1980, [Johnny Grant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johnny_Grant_\(radio_personality\) "Johnny Grant \(radio personality\)") was awarded a star for his television work,[[110]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-115) for which he took it upon himself to create a memorable ceremony. Impressed by his efforts, the Chamber and Grant agreed that he become Walk of Fame Committee chairman and that in this position he help resurrect the Walk.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70)[[111]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ap2006-116) Grant stimulated the Walk's publicity by requiring each new recipient to personally attend their star unveiling;[[111]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ap2006-116) he later recalled that "it was tough to get people to come accept a star" due to the state of the neighborhood at the time.[[108]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Vincent1-113) Grant also transformed the unveilings into media events commonly aired on nightly newscasts, and he instituted a $2,500 ($9,769in 2025) fee for each new star, to fund the Walk's upkeep.[[111]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-ap2006-116) The fee has increased over time and was $85,000 as of 2026[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit)[[70]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-WOFFAQforNoms-73)
In 2002, Grant received an Award of Excellence star to acknowledge his efforts to revitalize the neighborhood.[[20]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-JGrant-22)[[112]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-117) He was also named chairman of the Selection Committee and Honorary [Mayor of Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Hollywood "Mayor of Hollywood"),[[20]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-JGrant-22) the latter a ceremonial position previously held by [Art Linkletter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Linkletter "Art Linkletter") and [Monty Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monty_Hall "Monty Hall"),[[113]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-118)[[114]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-119) amongst others.[[115]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-vice_surreal-120) Grant held these positions until his death in 2008, by which point he had hosted more than 500 star unveilings[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70)[[116]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-121) and his name was considered synonymous with the Walk.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70) After Grant's death, representative [Diane Watson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diane_Watson "Diane Watson") stated that "no name [is] more associated with Hollywood’s Walk of Fame than Johnny Grant"[[117]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-122) and today, the Chamber credits Grant's leadership with having "built the Walk into an international icon."[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70)
### Preservation and expansion
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=14 "Edit section: Preservation and expansion")]
The Hollywood Walk of Fame was designated [Los Angeles Historic-Cultural Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Los_Angeles_Historic-Cultural_Monuments_in_Hollywood "List of Los Angeles Historic-Cultural Monuments in Hollywood") No. 194 on July 5, 1978.[[90]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LAHCM-94) In 1985, the [Hollywood Boulevard Commercial and Entertainment District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Boulevard_Commercial_and_Entertainment_District "Hollywood Boulevard Commercial and Entertainment District") was added to the [National Register of Historic Places](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Register_of_Historic_Places "National Register of Historic Places"), and while the Walk was not included in the listing, it was mentioned as an element of "fine urban design".[[91]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-HBCED-95)
[![Charcoal sidewalk featuring two rows of alternating stars, one row full and the other with gaps. No pedestrians.](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/82/Hollywood-walk-of-fame.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Hollywood-walk-of-fame.jpg)The Walk with two rows of alternating stars
In 1984, a fifth category, Live Theatre, was added to acknowledge contributions from the live performance branch of the entertainment industry.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70) In the 1990s, a second row of stars was created to alternate with stars from the first row.[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70) In 1994, the Walk was extended one block west,[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-historyof-70) to the _Hollywood and La Brea Gateway_ gazebo, which was added that same year;[[48]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-Fry-51)[[118]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-123) 33 honorees were anointed outside the Walk's normal nomination process during this expansion.[[73]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-reilly-76) Also in 1994, during construction of the [B Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B_Line_\(Los_Angeles_Metro\) "B Line \(Los Angeles Metro\)"), the [Metropolitan Transportation Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_Metropolitan_Transportation_Authority "Los Angeles County Metropolitan Transportation Authority") (MTA) removed and stored more than 200 stars.[[119]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-124) Controversy arose three years later, when the MTA proposed destroying the terrazzo pads of 122 additional stars while preserving only the brass lettering, surrounds, and medallions, then replacing the terrazzo after construction was complete,[[8]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-mta-8) but the [Cultural Heritage Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Historic-Cultural_Monument "Los Angeles Historic-Cultural Monument") ruled that the stars and their terrazzo pads needed to be removed intact.[[120]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-MTAdestruction-125)
The Walk celebrated its 50th anniversary in 2010. Each star unveiled that year was accompanied by a 50th anniversary plaque[[60]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-twincities-63) and a [time capsule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_capsule "Time capsule") was also added under its own star.[[55]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-time_capsule-58) Additionally, on July 25, 2010, the neighborhood held an anniversary festival that included live music, studio and theater tours, and an exhibit at the [Hollywood Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Museum "Hollywood Museum").[[121]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-126) The [U.S. House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._House_of_Representatives "U.S. House of Representatives") also officially commended and congratulated the Walk that year.[[74]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-houseofreps-77)
In 2023, a sixth category, Sports Entertainment, was added to acknowledge contributions of athletes to the entertainment industry.[[122]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-strahan-127) About 24 stars were added per year in all categories combined as of 2020[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit)[[61]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-janetlee-64) since then, more than 30 honorees have been selected every year but 2023.[[123]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-128)
### Restoration
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: Restoration")]
In 2007, the [Los Angeles Metropolitan Transit Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Metropolitan_Transit_Authority "Los Angeles Metropolitan Transit Authority") began a year-long study to determine the best way to stabilize the buckling that was damaging the Walk's stars and terrazzo.[[124]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-WOFfix-129) The following year, all 2,365 stars were evaluated, each of which received an [A, B, C, D, or F](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academic_grading_in_the_United_States "Academic grading in the United States"); 10 received Fs, 50 received Ds, and 778 (33% of all stars) were identified for replacement, as were 2,155 blank squares.[[125]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LAT22-130) The estimated cost for these replacements was $4.1 million ($6.13millionin 2025);[[124]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-WOFfix-129) however, this proved to be a severe underestimate.[[126]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-facelift-131) A two-year timeframe for the project[[125]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LAT22-130) was also an underestimate; star replacements did not begin until 2012[[126]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-facelift-131) and four years later, 20% of the Walk's stars remained in disrepair.[[84]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-baum-87)
[![closeup of a crack that extends horizontally above Michael Landon's name and across the top point of the star](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/Michael_Landon%27s_star_on_the_Walk_of_Fame.jpg/250px-Michael_Landon%27s_star_on_the_Walk_of_Fame.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Michael_Landon's_star_on_the_Walk_of_Fame.jpg)[Michael Landon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Landon "Michael Landon")'s star with cracks at its top, 2011
To encourage funding of the restoration by corporate sponsors, the Chamber inaugurated the "Friends of Walk of Fame" program in 2008.[[125]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LAT22-130) This program, which recognizes donors with honorary plaques near the Walk in front of [Dolby Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolby_Theatre "Dolby Theatre"),[[23]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LATAbs-25) received criticism, being described by a _[Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times "Los Angeles Times")_ reporter as "the latest corporate attempt to buy some good buzz." Another critic stated "I think Johnny Grant would roll over in his grave."[[23]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LATAbs-25) Corporations that participated in the program include [Absolut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absolut "Absolut") and [L'Oreal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L'Oreal "L'Oreal"),[[24]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-susko-26) with the sponsorships costing about $1 million each.[[127]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-132)
In 2018, city council-member [Mitch O'Farrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitch_O'Farrell "Mitch O'Farrell") initiated a plan to improve the entire Walk of Fame corridor; the following year, the [Los Angeles County Department of Public Works](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_Department_of_Public_Works "Los Angeles County Department of Public Works") in conjunction with the Hollywood Chamber of Commerce, [Hollywood Heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Heritage "Hollywood Heritage"), and the Hollywood Property Owners Alliance commissioned [Gensler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gensler "Gensler") to plan a $4 million ($5.04millionin 2025) renovation.[[128]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-133)[[129]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-134) Gensler's proposal was released the following year; it proposed new landscaping and bike lanes, sidewalk dining and widening, and the removal of street lanes and parking between Argyle Avenue and La Brea Avenue.[[130]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-masterplan-135)[[131]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-136) Streetscape improvements, including increasing sidewalk space; consolidating bus stops and adding bus shelters; adding benches, tables, and chairs for sidewalk dining; and removing parking lanes between Orange Drive and Gower Street, were approved in 2021 and funded in part by $7.2 million ($8.55millionin 2025) from [Measure M](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Measure_M "Measure M").[[132]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-137)
### Theft
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Theft")]
[![Gregory Peck's star on a vibrant charcoal terrazzo pad surrounded by faded terrazzo](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Los_Angeles_%28California%2C_USA%29%2C_Hollywood_Boulevard%2C_Gregory_Peck_--_2012_--_4975.jpg/250px-Los_Angeles_%28California%2C_USA%29%2C_Hollywood_Boulevard%2C_Gregory_Peck_--_2012_--_4975.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Los_Angeles_\(California,_USA\),_Hollywood_Boulevard,_Gregory_Peck_--_2012_--_4975.jpg)[Gregory Peck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregory_Peck "Gregory Peck")'s replacement star after the original was stolen in 2005
In 2000, [James Stewart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Stewart "James Stewart")'s and [Kirk Douglas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirk_Douglas "Kirk Douglas")' stars disappeared after they had been temporarily removed for a construction project. Police recovered the stars in the home of a construction worker employed on the project; however, the stars were badly damaged and had to be remade. One of [Gene Autry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene_Autry "Gene Autry")'s five stars was also stolen from a construction area.[[133]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-pooltheft-138)[[134]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-peckstar-139)
In 2005, thieves used a [concrete saw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concrete_saw "Concrete saw") to remove [Gregory Peck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregory_Peck "Gregory Peck")'s star from its site at Hollywood and North El Centro Avenue. The star was replaced almost immediately but the original was never found, nor were the perpetrators who stole it.[[133]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-pooltheft-138)[[134]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-peckstar-139) Another theft occurred in 2019, when Austin Clay, who had previously vandalized [Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Trump "Donald Trump")'s star, stole the [Marilyn Monroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marilyn_Monroe "Marilyn Monroe") statue atop _Hollywood and La Brea Gateway_.[[135]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-140)
Thieves have also attempted to chisel out individual stars' category emblems.[[133]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-pooltheft-138)
## Honorees
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: Honorees")]
Main article: [List of stars on the Hollywood Walk of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_stars_on_the_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame "List of stars on the Hollywood Walk of Fame")
See also: [List of actors with Hollywood Walk of Fame motion picture stars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_actors_with_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_motion_picture_stars "List of actors with Hollywood Walk of Fame motion picture stars")
While more than 2,850 stars have been awarded,[[2]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-wof_pr-2) because some individuals have been awarded multiple times, the number of individuals honored is not the same as the total star count. In the early 2010s, the _[Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times "Los Angeles Times")_ estimated that approximately 2,200 individuals or groups were honored by the more than 2,400 stars issued at that time.[[12]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LATabout-12)
### Diversity
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=18 "Edit section: Diversity")]
An analysis in 2011 found that while minority representation was rising, minorities were underrepresented on the Walk. Of all stars, 5.1% honored African-Americans, 3.4% honored Hispanics, and 0.4% honored Asians, all of which were significantly less than those minorities' percentage of the overall population.[[62]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-diversity-65) Analysts and commentators were not surprised by this, as the same lack of representation was present in [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_the_United_States "Cinema of the United States") as a whole.[[62]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-diversity-65)[[136]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-141) The [Hollywood Chamber of Commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Chamber_of_Commerce "Hollywood Chamber of Commerce") responded by stating that it has been working hard to improve diversity; they also noted that several of the Walk's original honorees were minorities, including [Anna May Wong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_May_Wong "Anna May Wong"), [Dolores Del Rio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolores_Del_Rio "Dolores Del Rio"), [Cantinflas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantinflas "Cantinflas"), and [Hattie McDaniel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hattie_McDaniel "Hattie McDaniel").[[62]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-diversity-65)
### Multiple honors
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame&action=edit&section=19 "Edit section: Multiple honors")]
[![Gene Autry live theater star with a rectangular plaque below it that reads: GENE AUTRY / The only individual honored with a star in all five categories of the Hollywood Walk of Fame / Radio · Motion Pictures · Recording · Television · Live Performance](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Gene_Autry_star_%28Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_stars%29_July_2023.JPG/250px-Gene_Autry_star_%28Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_stars%29_July_2023.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gene_Autry_star_\(Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_stars\)_July_2023.JPG)[Gene Autry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene_Autry "Gene Autry")'s star with a plaque noting him as the only individual with a star in all (at the time) five categories
[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Frank_Sinatra_star_for_Recording_at_1637_Vine_Street_on_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_20220402_150342_HDR_copy.jpg/250px-Frank_Sinatra_star_for_Recording_at_1637_Vine_Street_on_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_20220402_150342_HDR_copy.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Frank_Sinatra_star_for_Recording_at_1637_Vine_Street_on_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_20220402_150342_HDR_copy.jpg)
[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/37/Frank_Sinatra_star_for_Television_at_6538_Hollywood_Boulevard_on_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_20220402_145049_HDR_copy.jpg/250px-Frank_Sinatra_star_for_Television_at_6538_Hollywood_Boulevard_on_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_20220402_145049_HDR_copy.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Frank_Sinatra_star_for_Television_at_6538_Hollywood_Boulevard_on_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_20220402_145049_HDR_copy.jpg)
[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Frank_Sinatra_star_for_Motion_pictures_at_1600_Vine_Street_on_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_20220402_152954_HDR_copy.jpg/250px-Frank_Sinatra_star_for_Motion_pictures_at_1600_Vine_Street_on_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_20220402_152954_HDR_copy.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Frank_Sinatra_star_for_Motion_pictures_at_1600_Vine_Street_on_Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame_20220402_152954_HDR_copy.jpg)
[Frank Sinatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Sinatra "Frank Sinatra")'s three stars, for recording, television, and motion pictures
[Gene Autry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene_Autry "Gene Autry") is the only person honored in five categories, while [Bob Hope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Hope "Bob Hope") and [Tony Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Martin_\(American_singer\) "Tony Martin \(American singer\)") have been honored in four[[12]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-LATabout-12) and [Roy Rogers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roy_Rogers "Roy Rogers") and [Mickey Rooney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mickey_Rooney "Mickey Rooney") have been honored individually in three and in a shared star for a fourth, Roy Rogers as part of [Sons of the Pioneers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sons_of_the_Pioneers "Sons of the Pioneers")[[137]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-142) and Mickey Rooney with his wife [Jan Chamberlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Chamberlin "Jan Chamberlin").[[138]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame#cite_note-143) More than thirty individuals have been honored in three categories, including [Jack Benny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Benny "Jack Benny"), [Danny Kaye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danny_Kaye "Danny Kaye"), [Frank
